Then I’m starting the day feeling like I never actually rested, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively about everyday things?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it excessive, exactly. I mean, I do think about everyday stuff, like everyone does, but… I don’t know if it’s more than it should be. Maybe I’m not the best judge of that. What would count as excessive?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain situations or activities because of the way they make you feel?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not avoiding like I’m afraid of them, exactly. It’s more that I just don’t really want to do things I used to look forward to. Like pickup basketball, bowling nights, music festivals… even having people over and firing up the smoker. Lately I’ll cancel, or if I do show up, I’m kind of just there, going through the motions. I don’t know if that counts as avoidance, but… that’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel tired or lacking in energy?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe a little? I don’t want to overstate it. I still get through work and everything, so it’s not like I’m completely wiped out all the time. But yeah, I guess I do feel kind of drained more often than I’d like. Does that count?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but most days it’s like this heavy, gray mood just kind of follows me around — from the office to the car, then home. Even when nothing specific is going on, I’ll just feel… kind of hollow. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s been a while, I guess. Not just a couple weeks. Maybe about a year and a couple months? It kind of started around when the arguments at home started getting a lot worse. I probably should’ve paid attention sooner, but… yeah, around then.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in the pleasure you get from activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I still do some things, so it’s not like I’ve completely stopped living or anything. But stuff I used to really look forward to — pickup basketball, bowling nights, music festivals, having people over and firing up the smoker — lately I’ll either cancel, or I’ll go and just feel like I’m kind of… there, but not really enjoying it. More like I’m going through the motions. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess I have. I don’t want to make it sound like I do nothing anymore, because I still try to show up sometimes. But things I used to really look forward to — pickup basketball, bowling nights, music festivals, having friends over and firing up the smoker — lately I either cancel, or I’m there and it just feels like I’m going through the motions. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Has this feeling of being down and the loss of interest in activities been constant over the past year, or have you had periods where you felt better?\n\nPatient: It’s been mostly there, yeah… I mean, I’ve had better days here and there, so I don’t want to make it sound like every single minute has been awful. But overall, it’s kind of been going on since things at home started getting worse — about a year and two months now, I think. More like I’m going through the motions. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess I have. I don’t want to make it sound like I do nothing anymore, because I still try to show up sometimes. But things I used to really look forward to — pickup basketball, bowling nights, music festivals, having friends over and firing up the smoker — lately I either cancel, or I’m there and it just feels like I’m going through the motions. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Has this feeling of being down and the loss of interest in activities been constant over the past year, or have you had periods where you felt better?\n\nPatient: It’s been mostly there, yeah… I mean, I’ve had better days here and there, so I don’t want to make it sound like every single minute has been awful. But overall, it’s kind of been going on since things at home started getting worse — about a year and two months now, I think. Is that the kind of timeline you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have your feelings of being down or hollow been present for most of the day, every day, over the past year?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, I think. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi… thanks for seeing me. I guess what finally made me call the employee assistance program was my sleep. It’s probably not the biggest problem compared to what some people deal with, but I’m barely sleeping right lately. I lie there replaying work stuff and arguments at home, then I wake up around 3 or 4 and can’t really get back to sleep. I mean, I do think about everyday stuff, like everyone does, but… I don’t know if it’s more than it should be. Maybe I’m not the best judge of that. What would count as excessive?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain situations or activities because of the way they make you feel?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not avoiding like I’m afraid of them, exactly. It’s more that I just don’t really want to do things I used to look forward to. Like pickup basketball, bowling nights, music festivals… even having people over and firing up the smoker. Lately I’ll cancel, or if I do show up, I’m kind of just there, going through the motions. I don’t know if that counts as avoidance, but… that’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel tired or lacking in energy?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe a little? I don’t want to overstate it. I still get through work and everything, so it’s not like I’m completely wiped out all the time. But yeah, I guess I do feel kind of drained more often than I’d like. Does that count?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but most days it’s like this heavy, gray mood just kind of follows me around — from the office to the car, then home. Even when nothing specific is going on, I’ll just feel… kind of hollow. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s been a while, I guess. Not just a couple weeks. Maybe about a year and a couple months? It kind of started around when the arguments at home started getting a lot worse. I probably should’ve paid attention sooner, but… yeah, around then.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in the pleasure you get from activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I still do some things, so it’s not like I’ve completely stopped living or anything. But stuff I used to really look forward to — pickup basketball, bowling nights, music festivals, having people over and firing up the smoker — lately I’ll either cancel, or I’ll go and just feel like I’m kind of… there, but not really enjoying it. More like I’m going through the motions. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess I have. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Has this feeling of being down and the loss of interest in activities been constant over the past year, or have you had periods where you felt better?\n\nPatient: It’s been mostly there, yeah… I mean, I’ve had better days here and there, so I don’t want to make it sound like every single minute has been awful. But overall, it’s kind of been going on since things at home started getting worse — about a year and two months now, I think. Is that the kind of timeline you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have your feelings of being down or hollow been present for most of the day, every day, over the past year?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, I think. I don’t want to overstate it like it’s dramatic every single second, but it’s kind of this heavy, gray feeling that just follows me around — from the office to the car to the house. Even when nothing specific is going wrong in that moment, I’ll still feel sort of… hollow. Does that answer it the right way?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this way for more than two years?\n\nPatient: No, not more than two years. It’s been… about a year and two months, I’d say, since the arguments at home started getting a lot worse. So it’s been a while, but not that long. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of at least two months where you didn't feel down or hollow over the past year and two months?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. Most days it’s still that heavy, gray feeling following me around… even when nothing in particular is happening, I just feel kind of hollow. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Has there been any time over the past year and two months when you felt completely back to your normal self for at least a couple of weeks?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I mean, I’ve had better days here and there, but I wouldn’t say I felt completely back to my normal self for a couple of weeks. It’s been going on about a year and two months now, since the arguments at home got worse. I guess it’s been pretty steady, even if I try not to make a big deal out of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a noticeable decrease in pleasure from activities that normally would bring you joy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I still try to do things, so I don’t want to make it sound like I’ve completely stopped living or anything. But stuff I used to really look forward to—pickup basketball, bowling nights, music festivals, having friends over and firing up the smoker—lately I either cancel, or I go and I’m kind of just… there. Going through the motions more than actually enjoying it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you had at least two weeks where you felt consistently down or lost interest in activities nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s fair to say. I don’t want to overstate it, but most days it’s like this heavy, gray mood just follows me around—from the office to the car to the house. Even when nothing specific is wrong, I just feel kind of hollow. So… yes, I guess it has been pretty consistent.\n\nDoctor: Have there been any significant changes or stressors in your life, aside from the arguments at home, that you think might be contributing to how you've been feeling?\n\nPatient: Um… aside from the arguments at home, I can’t really point to anything big. Work is work, you know, sales can be stressful, but nothing that feels unusual or like a major change. I’ve kind of been trying to figure that out myself, honestly, because I don’t want to blame everything on one thing if that’s not right. But no, nothing significant really comes to mind.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It’s more that I just don’t really want to do things I used to look forward to. Like pickup basketball, bowling nights, music festivals… even having people over and firing up the smoker. Lately I’ll cancel, or if I do show up, I’m kind of just there, going through the motions. I don’t know if that counts as avoidance, but… that’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel tired or lacking in energy?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe a little? I don’t want to overstate it. I still get through work and everything, so it’s not like I’m completely wiped out all the time. But yeah, I guess I do feel kind of drained more often than I’d like. Does that count?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but most days it’s like this heavy, gray mood just kind of follows me around — from the office to the car, then home. Even when nothing specific is going on, I’ll just feel… kind of hollow. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s been a while, I guess. Not just a couple weeks. Maybe about a year and a couple months? It kind of started around when the arguments at home started getting a lot worse. I probably should’ve paid attention sooner, but… yeah, around then.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in the pleasure you get from activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I still do some things, so it’s not like I’ve completely stopped living or anything. But stuff I used to really look forward to — pickup basketball, bowling nights, music festivals, having people over and firing up the smoker — lately I’ll either cancel, or I’ll go and just feel like I’m kind of… there, but not really enjoying it. More like I’m going through the motions. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess I have. I don’t want to make it sound like I do nothing anymore, because I still try to show up sometimes. But things I used to really look forward to — pickup basketball, bowling nights, music festivals, having friends over and firing up the smoker — lately I either cancel, or I’m there and it just feels like I’m going through the motions. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Has this feeling of being down and the loss of interest in activities been constant over the past year, or have you had periods where you felt better?\n\nPatient: It’s been mostly there, yeah… I mean, I’ve had better days here and there, so I don’t want to make it sound like every single minute has been awful. But overall, it’s kind of been going on since things at home started getting worse — about a year and two months now, I think. Is that the kind of timeline you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have your feelings of being down or hollow been present for most of the day, every day, over the past year?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, I think. I don’t want to overstate it like it’s dramatic every single second, but it’s kind of this heavy, gray feeling that just follows me around — from the office to the car to the house. Even when nothing specific is going wrong in that moment, I’ll still feel sort of… hollow. Does that answer it the right way?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this way for more than two years?\n\nPatient: No, not more than two years. It’s been… about a year and two months, I’d say, since the arguments at home started getting a lot worse. So it’s been a while, but not that long. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of at least two months where you didn't feel down or hollow over the past year and two months?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I’ve had stretches where I functioned okay or maybe felt a little lighter for a few days, but not, like, two whole months where it was gone. Most days it’s still that heavy, gray feeling following me around… even when nothing in particular is happening, I just feel kind of hollow. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Has there been any time over the past year and two months when you felt completely back to your normal self for at least a couple of weeks?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I mean, I’ve had better days here and there, but I wouldn’t say I felt completely back to my normal self for a couple of weeks. It’s been going on about a year and two months now, since the arguments at home got worse. Going through the motions more than actually enjoying it. I’ve kind of been trying to figure that out myself, honestly, because I don’t want to blame everything on one thing if that’s not right. But no, nothing significant really comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you had at least five months in the past year and two months where you felt consistently down or had a loss of interest in activities nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess if I’m being honest, it’s probably been about that long. I don’t know if that’s the kind of timeframe you mean.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I don’t know if that counts as avoidance, but… that’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel tired or lacking in energy?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe a little? I don’t want to overstate it. I still get through work and everything, so it’s not like I’m completely wiped out all the time. But yeah, I guess I do feel kind of drained more often than I’d like. Does that count?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but most days it’s like this heavy, gray mood just kind of follows me around — from the office to the car, then home. Even when nothing specific is going on, I’ll just feel… kind of hollow. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s been a while, I guess. Not just a couple weeks. Maybe about a year and a couple months? It kind of started around when the arguments at home started getting a lot worse. I probably should’ve paid attention sooner, but… yeah, around then.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in the pleasure you get from activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I still do some things, so it’s not like I’ve completely stopped living or anything. But stuff I used to really look forward to — pickup basketball, bowling nights, music festivals, having people over and firing up the smoker — lately I’ll either cancel, or I’ll go and just feel like I’m kind of… there, but not really enjoying it. More like I’m going through the motions. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess I have. I don’t want to make it sound like I do nothing anymore, because I still try to show up sometimes. But things I used to really look forward to — pickup basketball, bowling nights, music festivals, having friends over and firing up the smoker — lately I either cancel, or I’m there and it just feels like I’m going through the motions. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Has this feeling of being down and the loss of interest in activities been constant over the past year, or have you had periods where you felt better?\n\nPatient: It’s been mostly there, yeah… I mean, I’ve had better days here and there, so I don’t want to make it sound like every single minute has been awful. But overall, it’s kind of been going on since things at home started getting worse — about a year and two months now, I think. Is that the kind of timeline you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have your feelings of being down or hollow been present for most of the day, every day, over the past year?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, I think. I don’t want to overstate it like it’s dramatic every single second, but it’s kind of this heavy, gray feeling that just follows me around — from the office to the car to the house. Even when nothing specific is going wrong in that moment, I’ll still feel sort of… hollow. Does that answer it the right way?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this way for more than two years?\n\nPatient: No, not more than two years. It’s been… about a year and two months, I’d say, since the arguments at home started getting a lot worse. So it’s been a while, but not that long. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of at least two months where you didn't feel down or hollow over the past year and two months?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I’ve had stretches where I functioned okay or maybe felt a little lighter for a few days, but not, like, two whole months where it was gone. Most days it’s still that heavy, gray feeling following me around… even when nothing in particular is happening, I just feel kind of hollow. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Has there been any time over the past year and two months when you felt completely back to your normal self for at least a couple of weeks?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I mean, I’ve had better days here and there, but I wouldn’t say I felt completely back to my normal self for a couple of weeks. It’s been going on about a year and two months now, since the arguments at home got worse. I guess it’s been pretty steady, even if I try not to make a big deal out of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a noticeable decrease in pleasure from activities that normally would bring you joy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I still try to do things, so I don’t want to make it sound like I’ve completely stopped living or anything. But stuff I used to really look forward to—pickup basketball, bowling nights, music festivals, having friends over and firing up the smoker—lately I either cancel, or I go and I’m kind of just… there. Going through the motions more than actually enjoying it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you had at least two weeks where you felt consistently down or lost interest in activities nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s fair to say. I don’t want to overstate it, but most days it’s like this heavy, gray mood just follows me around—from the office to the car to the house. Even when nothing specific is wrong, I just feel kind of hollow. So… yes, I guess it has been pretty consistent.\n\nDoctor: Have there been any significant changes or stressors in your life, aside from the arguments at home, that you think might be contributing to how you've been feeling?\n\nPatient: Um… aside from the arguments at home, I can’t really point to anything big. Work is work, you know, sales can be stressful, but nothing that feels unusual or like a major change. I’ve kind of been trying to figure that out myself, honestly, because I don’t want to blame everything on one thing if that’s not right. But no, nothing significant really comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you had at least five months in the past year and two months where you felt consistently down or had a loss of interest in activities nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess if I’m being honest, it’s probably been about that long. Maybe around a year and two months. It kind of started when the arguments at home got a lot worse, and since then it’s been… not every single second, but pretty close to most days. I don’t know if that’s the kind of timeframe you mean.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy and look forward to?\n\nPatient: I mean… I still want to enjoy things, if that makes sense. I used to really look forward to pickup basketball, bowling nights, going to music festivals, even having people over and firing up the smoker. Lately, though, I’ll either cancel or I’ll go and… I’m there, but it feels like I’m just going through the motions. It’s not like I hate those things now, but I don’t get that same feeling from them. Is that what you’re asking?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

It’s more that I just don’t really want to do things I used to look forward to. Like pickup basketball, bowling nights, music festivals… even having people over and firing up the smoker. Lately I’ll cancel, or if I do show up, I’m kind of just there, going through the motions. I don’t know if that counts as avoidance, but… that’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel tired or lacking in energy?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe a little? I don’t want to overstate it. I still get through work and everything, so it’s not like I’m completely wiped out all the time. But yeah, I guess I do feel kind of drained more often than I’d like. Does that count?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but most days it’s like this heavy, gray mood just kind of follows me around — from the office to the car, then home. Even when nothing specific is going on, I’ll just feel… kind of hollow. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s been a while, I guess. Not just a couple weeks. Maybe about a year and a couple months? It kind of started around when the arguments at home started getting a lot worse. I probably should’ve paid attention sooner, but… yeah, around then.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in the pleasure you get from activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I still do some things, so it’s not like I’ve completely stopped living or anything. But stuff I used to really look forward to — pickup basketball, bowling nights, music festivals, having people over and firing up the smoker — lately I’ll either cancel, or I’ll go and just feel like I’m kind of… there, but not really enjoying it. More like I’m going through the motions. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess I have. I don’t want to make it sound like I do nothing anymore, because I still try to show up sometimes. But things I used to really look forward to — pickup basketball, bowling nights, music festivals, having friends over and firing up the smoker — lately I either cancel, or I’m there and it just feels like I’m going through the motions. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Has this feeling of being down and the loss of interest in activities been constant over the past year, or have you had periods where you felt better?\n\nPatient: It’s been mostly there, yeah… I mean, I’ve had better days here and there, so I don’t want to make it sound like every single minute has been awful. But overall, it’s kind of been going on since things at home started getting worse — about a year and two months now, I think. Is that the kind of timeline you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have your feelings of being down or hollow been present for most of the day, every day, over the past year?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, I think. I don’t want to overstate it like it’s dramatic every single second, but it’s kind of this heavy, gray feeling that just follows me around — from the office to the car to the house. Even when nothing specific is going wrong in that moment, I’ll still feel sort of… hollow. Does that answer it the right way?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this way for more than two years?\n\nPatient: No, not more than two years. It’s been… about a year and two months, I’d say, since the arguments at home started getting a lot worse. So it’s been a while, but not that long. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of at least two months where you didn't feel down or hollow over the past year and two months?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I’ve had stretches where I functioned okay or maybe felt a little lighter for a few days, but not, like, two whole months where it was gone. Most days it’s still that heavy, gray feeling following me around… even when nothing in particular is happening, I just feel kind of hollow. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Has there been any time over the past year and two months when you felt completely back to your normal self for at least a couple of weeks?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I mean, I’ve had better days here and there, but I wouldn’t say I felt completely back to my normal self for a couple of weeks. It’s been going on about a year and two months now, since the arguments at home got worse. I guess it’s been pretty steady, even if I try not to make a big deal out of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a noticeable decrease in pleasure from activities that normally would bring you joy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I still try to do things, so I don’t want to make it sound like I’ve completely stopped living or anything. But stuff I used to really look forward to—pickup basketball, bowling nights, music festivals, having friends over and firing up the smoker—lately I either cancel, or I go and I’m kind of just… there. Going through the motions more than actually enjoying it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you had at least two weeks where you felt consistently down or lost interest in activities nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s fair to say. I don’t want to overstate it, but most days it’s like this heavy, gray mood just follows me around—from the office to the car to the house. Even when nothing specific is wrong, I just feel kind of hollow. So… yes, I guess it has been pretty consistent.\n\nDoctor: Have there been any significant changes or stressors in your life, aside from the arguments at home, that you think might be contributing to how you've been feeling?\n\nPatient: Um… aside from the arguments at home, I can’t really point to anything big. Work is work, you know, sales can be stressful, but nothing that feels unusual or like a major change. I’ve kind of been trying to figure that out myself, honestly, because I don’t want to blame everything on one thing if that’s not right. But no, nothing significant really comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you had at least five months in the past year and two months where you felt consistently down or had a loss of interest in activities nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess if I’m being honest, it’s probably been about that long. Maybe around a year and two months. It kind of started when the arguments at home got a lot worse, and since then it’s been… not every single second, but pretty close to most days. I don’t know if that’s the kind of timeframe you mean.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy and look forward to?\n\nPatient: I mean… I still want to enjoy things, if that makes sense. I used to really look forward to pickup basketball, bowling nights, going to music festivals, even having people over and firing up the smoker. Lately, though, I’ll either cancel or I’ll go and… I’m there, but it feels like I’m just going through the motions. It’s not like I hate those things now, but I don’t get that same feeling from them. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt this way for at least two years?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s been two years. It’s been more like… about a year and two months, I’d say, since the arguments at home started getting a lot worse. So not quite that long, if I’m understanding the question right.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
